title: "Kernel API reference"

description: "API reference for the Kernel plugin in Corsair: every `kernel.api.*` operation with its input and output types, taken from the plugin's Zod schemas."
---

<Info>
**New to Corsair?** See [API access](/concepts/api), [authentication](/concepts/auth), and [error handling](/concepts/error-handling).
</Info>

## Api Keys

### create

`apiKeys.create`

Create a Kernel API key. The secret is shown once in the response: store it right away and never paste it into chat.

**Risk:** `destructive`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apiKeys.create({ name: 'my-name' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `days_to_expire` | `number \| null` | No | Number of days until expiry, up to 3650. Use null for never. Cannot be combined with expires_at. |
| `expires_at` | `string \| null` | No | Exact expiry time. Must be in the future and no later than the issuing credential's own expiry. Cannot be combined with days_to_expire. |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | Label for the API key (1-255 characters). API keys are not addressable by name. |
| `policy` | `object` | No | — |
| `project_id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique project identifier |
| `service_account_id` | `string` | No | Link to an active same-organization service account. Requires an organization administrator, explicit project and nonempty policy. Omit for a personal key. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="policy full type">

```ts
{
  statements: {
    action: string,
    condition?: {
      key: source_ip,
      operator: in,
      values: string[]
    },
    resource: string
  }[]
}
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the API key was created |
| `created_by` | `object \| null` | No | Human audit creator, or null after creator deletion. Not the service credential's principal. |
| `deleted_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key was deleted (soft-deleted). Null for keys that have not been deleted. |
| `expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key expires |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique API key identifier |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key last authenticated a request, accurate to within about 20 minutes. Null if the key has not been used since last-use tracking began. |
| `masked_key` | `string \| null` | No | Masked version of the API key |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Label for the API key. API keys are not addressable by name; use the ID or key identifier for stable references. |
| `policy` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `principal` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `project_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project identifier for project-scoped API keys. Null means org-wide. |
| `project_name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project name for project-scoped API keys. Null means the key is org-wide or the project name is unavailable. |
| `service_account_id` | `string \| null` | No | Organization-owned service principal, when linked. |
| `key` | `string \| null` | No | Plaintext API key. Only returned once when the key is created. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="created_by full type">

```ts
{
  email?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  name?: string | null | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="policy full type">

```ts
{
  statements?: {
    action?: string | null,
    condition?: {
      key?: string | null,
      operator?: string | null,
      values?: string[] | null
    } | null,
    resource?: string | null
  }[] | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="principal full type">

```ts
{
  id?: string | null,
  type?: string | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

### delete

`apiKeys.delete`

Delete an API key; anything using it stops working. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apiKeys.delete({ id: 'apiKey_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | API key ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### get

`apiKeys.get`

Get an API key's metadata.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apiKeys.get({ id: 'apiKey_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | API key ID |
| `include_deleted` | `boolean` | No | When true, return the API key even if it has been deleted (soft-deleted), for audit purposes. Defaults to false, which returns 404 for a deleted key. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the API key was created |
| `created_by` | `object \| null` | No | Human audit creator, or null after creator deletion. Not the service credential's principal. |
| `deleted_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key was deleted (soft-deleted). Null for keys that have not been deleted. |
| `expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key expires |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique API key identifier |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key last authenticated a request, accurate to within about 20 minutes. Null if the key has not been used since last-use tracking began. |
| `masked_key` | `string \| null` | No | Masked version of the API key |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Label for the API key. API keys are not addressable by name; use the ID or key identifier for stable references. |
| `policy` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `principal` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `project_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project identifier for project-scoped API keys. Null means org-wide. |
| `project_name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project name for project-scoped API keys. Null means the key is org-wide or the project name is unavailable. |
| `service_account_id` | `string \| null` | No | Organization-owned service principal, when linked. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="created_by full type">

```ts
{
  email?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  name?: string | null | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="policy full type">

```ts

  statements?: {
    action?: string | null,
    condition?: {
      key?: string | null,
      operator?: string | null,
      values?: string[] | null
    } | null,
    resource?: string | null
  }[] | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

  id?: string | null,

### list

`apiKeys.list`

List API keys (names, scopes, last used; never the secrets).

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apiKeys.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `limit` | `number` | No | Maximum number of results to return |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Number of results to skip |
| `query` | `string` | No | Case-insensitive substring match against API key name, creator, and project. API key identifiers and masked keys match by exact value or prefix. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Exact-match filter on API key name using the database collation. In production, matching is case- and accent-insensitive. Names are not required to be unique, so multiple keys may match. When status=all or include_deleted=true is set, soft-deleted keys with the same name may also match. |
| `sort_by` | `created_at \| name \| expires_at \| last_used_at` | No | Field to sort API keys by. Keys with no expiry or no recorded use sort last for expires_at and last_used_at in either direction. |
| `sort_direction` | `asc \| desc` | No | Sort direction for API keys. |
| `status` | `active \| expired \| deleted \| all` | No | Filter API keys by status. "active" returns keys that are neither deleted nor expired (default), "expired" returns keys that are not deleted and whose expiry has passed, "deleted" returns only soft-deleted keys, "all" returns every key. |
| `service_account_id` | `string` | No | Return only keys that belong to this service account. |
| `principal_type` | `user \| service_account` | No | Return only keys that act as a user, or only keys that act as a service account. |
| `created_by` | `string` | No | Return only keys created by this user ID. This is the same person the key's created_by field describes. |
| `project_id` | `string` | No | Return only keys scoped to this project ID. The value "org" returns keys that are not scoped to a project. |
| `include_deleted` | `boolean` | No | Deprecated: use status=all instead. When true, include deleted (soft-deleted) API keys in the results for audit purposes. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  created_at?: string | null,
  created_by?: {
    email?: string | null,
    id?: string | null,
    name?: string | null | null
  } | null | null,
  deleted_at?: string | null | null,
  expires_at?: string | null | null,
  id?: string | null,
  last_used_at?: string | null | null,
  masked_key?: string | null,
  name?: string | null,
  policy?: {
    statements?: {
      action?: string | null,
      condition?: {
        key?: string | null,
        operator?: string | null,
        values?: string[] | null
      } | null,
      resource?: string | null
    }[] | null
  } | null | null,
  principal?: {
    id?: string | null,
    type?: string | null
  } | null,
  project_id?: string | null | null,
  project_name?: string | null | null,
  service_account_id?: string | null
}[]
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

### rotate

`apiKeys.rotate`

Replace an API key's secret; the old secret stops working. The new one is shown once.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apiKeys.rotate({ id: 'apiKey_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | API key ID |
| `days_to_expire` | `number \| null` | No | Lifetime in days for the new key, up to 3650. Omit to reuse the rotated key's original lifetime, or never-expires if it had none. |
| `expire_in_days` | `number \| null` | No | Grace period in days before the rotated key expires. Use 0 to expire it immediately. Omit for the default grace period of 7 days. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the API key was created |
| `created_by` | `object \| null` | No | Human audit creator, or null after creator deletion. Not the service credential's principal. |
| `deleted_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key was deleted (soft-deleted). Null for keys that have not been deleted. |
| `expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key expires |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique API key identifier |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the API key last authenticated a request, accurate to within about 20 minutes. Null if the key has not been used since last-use tracking began. |
| `masked_key` | `string \| null` | No | Masked version of the API key |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Label for the API key. API keys are not addressable by name; use the ID or key identifier for stable references. |
| `policy` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `principal` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `project_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project identifier for project-scoped API keys. Null means org-wide. |
| `project_name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Project name for project-scoped API keys. Null means the key is org-wide or the project name is unavailable. |
| `service_account_id` | `string \| null` | No | Organization-owned service principal, when linked. |
| `key` | `string \| null` | No | Plaintext API key. Only returned once when the key is created. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="created_by full type">

```ts
{
  email?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  name?: string | null | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="policy full type">

```ts
{
  statements?: {
    action?: string | null,
    condition?: {
      key?: string | null,
      operator?: string | null,
      values?: string[] | null
    } | null,
    resource?: string | null
  }[] | null
} | null | null
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="principal full type">

```ts
{
  id?: string | null,
  type?: string | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

## Apps

### list

`apps.list`

List deployed Kernel apps and their versions and actions.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.apps.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `app_name` | `string` | No | Filter results by application name. |
| `version` | `string` | No | Filter results by version label. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Limit the number of apps to return. |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Offset the number of apps to return. |
| `query` | `string` | No | Search apps by name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  actions?: {
    input_schema?: {
    } | null | null,
    name?: string | null,
    output_schema?: {
    } | null | null
  }[] | null,
  app_name?: string | null,
  deployment?: string | null,
  env_vars?: Record<string, string> | null,
  id?: string | null,
  region?: string | null,
  version?: string | null
}[]
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

## Audit Logs

### exportChunk

`auditLogs.exportChunk`

Download one chunk of the audit log as JSONL (gzipped by default). Returns a file plus has_more and next_cursor for the next chunk.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auditLogs.exportChunk({ start: '2026-11-19T00:00:00Z', end: '2026-11-20T00:00:00Z', format: 'jsonl' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `start` | `string` | Yes | Lower bound (inclusive) for the audit record timestamp. |
| `end` | `string` | Yes | Upper bound (exclusive) for the audit record timestamp. |
| `auth_strategy` | `string` | No | Filter by authentication strategy. |
| `service` | `string` | No | Filter by service name. |
| `method` | `string` | No | Filter by HTTP method. |
| `exclude_method` | `string[]` | No | Filter out results by HTTP method. |
| `search` | `string` | No | Free-text search over path, user ID, email, client IP, and status. |
| `search_user_id` | `string[]` | No | Additional user IDs to OR into free-text search. |
| `cursor` | `string` | No | Opaque cursor from X-Next-Cursor for the next chunk of older records. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Maximum number of records to return in this chunk. |
| `format` | `jsonl \| jsonl.gz` | No | Encoding for the returned chunk. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when another chunk follows; pass next_cursor as cursor to read it. |
| `next_cursor` | `string \| null` | Yes | Pass as cursor to read the next chunk. |



---

### exportDestinations.create

`auditLogs.exportDestinations.create`

Add a destination (such as an S3 bucket) that receives continuous audit log exports.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auditLogs.exportDestinations.create({
	type: 's3',
	region: 'example',
	bucket: 'example',
	prefix: 'example',
	role_arn: 'example',
	format: 'jsonl.gz',
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `bucket` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `format` | `jsonl.gz` | Yes | — |
| `kms_key_id` | `string` | No | — |
| `prefix` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `region` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `role_arn` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `type` | `s3` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `bucket` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `consecutive_failures` | `number \| null` | No | — |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `external_id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `format` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `kernel_role_arn` | `string \| null` | No | The Kernel role that assumes `role_arn` in your account to deliver logs. Allow this role as the principal in your role's trust policy, and require `external_id` as the `sts:ExternalId` condition. Recreating a destination issues a new `external_id`, which the trust policy has to be updated to match. |
| `kms_key_id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `last_error` | `string \| null` | No | Sanitized description of the most recent delivery failure. |
| `last_error_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `last_exported_cursor` | `string \| null` | No | Opaque, versioned checkpoint for forward-only continuous export. This value is not compatible with audit-log list page tokens. Delivery starts at the moment the destination is activated, so events recorded before that are not delivered. Pausing stops delivery and resuming starts again from the time of the resume: events recorded while a destination was paused are never exported, and pausing is not a way to defer delivery. |
| `last_success_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `next_attempt_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `prefix` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `region` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `role_arn` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Pausing prevents new delivery attempts. An S3 upload already in progress may complete after the pause response; its rows can appear again after the destination is resumed. |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |

Allow this role as the principal in your role's trust policy, and require `external_id` as the `sts:ExternalId` condition. Recreating a destination issues a new `external_id`, which the trust policy has to be updated to match. |

Recreating a destination issues a new `external_id`, which the trust policy has to be updated to match. |

Delivery starts at the moment the destination is activated, so events recorded before that are not delivered. Pausing stops delivery and resuming starts again from the time of the resume: events recorded while a destination was paused are never exported, and pausing is not a way to defer delivery. |

### list

`auditLogs.list`

Search the org's audit log (who called what, when), newest first. Pages with page_token; check has_more.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auditLogs.list({ start: '2026-11-19T00:00:00Z', end: '2026-11-20T00:00:00Z', limit: 50 });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `start` | `string` | Yes | Lower bound (inclusive) for the audit record timestamp. |
| `end` | `string` | Yes | Upper bound (exclusive) for the audit record timestamp. |
| `auth_strategy` | `string` | No | Filter by authentication strategy. |
| `service` | `string` | No | Filter by service name. |
| `method` | `string` | No | Filter by HTTP method. |
| `exclude_method` | `string[]` | No | Filter out results by HTTP method. |
| `search` | `string` | No | Free-text search over path, user ID, email, client IP, and status. |
| `search_user_id` | `string[]` | No | Additional user IDs to OR into free-text search. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Maximum number of results to return. |
| `page_token` | `string` | No | Opaque page token from X-Next-Page-Token for the next page of older records. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_page_token as page_token to read them. |
| `next_page_token` | `string \| null` | Yes | Value for `page_token` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

## Auth

### connections.create

`auth.connections.create`

Set up managed login for a site: one connection per profile and domain (the profile is created if missing). Then start a login with auth.connections.login.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.create({ domain: 'app.acme.com', profile_name: 'acme-login' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `allowed_domains` | `string[]` | No | Additional hostname roots valid for this auth flow, besides the primary domain. Each value allows credential entry on that exact hostname and its subdomains. Leading `www.` and `*.` labels are normalized away. When omitted or empty, credential entry is unrestricted. The following SSO/OAuth provider domains are automatically allowed by default and do not need to be specified: - Google: accounts.google.com - Microsoft/Azure AD: login.microsoftonline.com, login.live.com - Okta: *.okta.com, *.oktapreview.com - Auth0: *.auth0.com, *.us.auth0.com, *.eu.auth0.com, *.au.auth0.com - Apple: appleid.apple.com - GitHub: github.com - Facebook/Meta: www.facebook.com - LinkedIn: www.linkedin.com - Amazon Cognito: *.amazoncognito.com - OneLogin: *.onelogin.com - Ping Identity: *.pingone.com, *.pingidentity.com |
| `auto_reauth` | `boolean` | No | Whether to permit automatic re-authentication when a scheduled health check detects an expired session. This is an opt-in flag only — it does not check whether re-auth is actually feasible. Even when true, re-auth only runs when the system has what it needs to perform it (for example, saved credentials for the required login fields), and only after a scheduled health check detects an expired session — so this flag has no effect when `health_checks` is false. When false, expired sessions are marked as `NEEDS_AUTH` instead of attempting re-auth. Defaults to true. |
| `browser` | `object` | No | — |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `credential` | `object` | No | — |
| `domain` | `string` | Yes | Domain for authentication |
| `health_check_interval` | `number` | No | Interval in seconds between automatic health checks. When set, the system periodically verifies the authentication status and triggers re-authentication if needed. Maximum is 86400 (24 hours). Default is 3600 (1 hour) or your plan minimum, whichever is larger. The minimum depends on your plan: Enterprise: 300 (5 minutes), Startup: 1200 (20 minutes), Hobbyist: 3600 (1 hour), Free: 21600 (6 hours). |
| `health_checks` | `boolean` | No | Whether to enable periodic health checks. When false, the system will not automatically verify authentication status, and `auto_reauth` has no effect on the automatic flow (since re-auth is only triggered by a failed scheduled health check). Defaults to true. |
| `hitl` | `boolean` | No | Whether a login blocked by a CAPTCHA the browser cannot solve pauses for a person instead of failing. The flow moves to AWAITING_HUMAN_INTERVENTION with a `human_intervention` choice; the person completes the challenge in `live_view_url`, then submits that choice to resume. Reauthentication never pauses. Defaults to false. |
| `login_url` | `string` | No | Optional login page URL to skip discovery |
| `profile_name` | `string` | Yes | Name of the profile to manage authentication for. If the profile does not exist, it is created automatically. |
| `proxy` | `object` | No | — |
| `record_session` | `boolean` | No | Whether to record browser sessions for this connection by default. Useful for debugging. Can be overridden per-login. Defaults to false. |
| `save_credentials` | `boolean` | No | Whether to save credentials after every successful login. Defaults to true. One-time codes (TOTP, SMS, etc.) are not saved. |

**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `allowed_domains` | `string[] \| null` | No | Additional hostname roots valid for this auth flow, besides the primary domain. Each value allows credential entry on that exact hostname and its subdomains. Leading `www.` and `*.` labels are normalized away. When omitted or empty, credential entry is unrestricted. The following SSO/OAuth provider domains are automatically allowed by default and do not need to be specified: - Google: accounts.google.com - Microsoft/Azure AD: login.microsoftonline.com, login.live.com - Okta: *.okta.com, *.oktapreview.com - Auth0: *.auth0.com, *.us.auth0.com, *.eu.auth0.com, *.au.auth0.com - Apple: appleid.apple.com - GitHub: github.com - Facebook/Meta: www.facebook.com - LinkedIn: www.linkedin.com - Amazon Cognito: *.amazoncognito.com - OneLogin: *.onelogin.com - Ping Identity: *.pingone.com, *.pingidentity.com |
| `auto_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether automatic re-authentication is permitted for this connection. This is an opt-in flag only — it does not check whether re-auth is actually feasible. Even when true, re-auth only runs when the system has what it needs to perform it (for example, saved credentials for the required login fields), and only after a scheduled health check detects an expired session — so this flag has no effect when `health_checks` is false. When false, expired sessions detected by a health check are marked as `NEEDS_AUTH` instead of attempting re-auth. |
| `browser` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `browser_session_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | ID of the underlying browser session driving the current flow (present when flow in progress). Use this to inspect or terminate the browser session via the `/browsers` API. |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `can_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether this connection's stored requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication. A true value can represent either fully satisfiable requirements or a best-effort attempt. It does not account for whether automatic re-authentication is enabled or parent workflow state such as an active flow or circuit-breaker cooldown, so it does not guarantee an attempt on the next health check. See `can_reauth_reason` for the specific outcome. |
| `can_reauth_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Machine-readable reason for the current value of `can_reauth`. Affirmative values (requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication): - `external_credential` — an external credential provider is attached - `cua_has_credential` — CUA flow with a stored credential - `has_credential` — Kernel credential is attached (optimistic; plan viability not checked) - `viable_plans_found` — at least one stored login plan can be replayed - `no_requirements_recorded` — no recorded credential requirements to fail against - `totp_reauth_allowed` — TOTP is the only recorded requirement and a stored secret can generate the code - `optimistic_totp_attempt` — TOTP was previously required but no reusable secret is stored; the connection remains eligible for a circuit-breaker-bounded attempt because the site may not challenge returning sessions - `requirements_satisfiable` — recorded requirements contain no recognized blocker Negative values (a human must complete the login flow): - `no_prior_successful_login` — connection has never completed a successful login - `no_credential` — no Kernel or external credential attached - `no_viable_plans` — credential attached but no replayable login plan exists yet - `viable_plans_require_external_action` — stored plans need an external step (email link, push, etc.) - `requires_external_action` — recorded requirements include an external step - `requires_totp_without_secret` — flow needs a TOTP code but no TOTP secret is stored - `requires_sms_code` — flow needs an SMS code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_email_code` — flow needs an email code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_customer_input` — flow needs another field or choice that is unavailable during unattended re-authentication |
| `choices` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical choices awaiting selection. Prefer this over pending_sso_buttons, mfa_options, and sign_in_options when present. |
| `credential` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `discovered_fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Fields awaiting input (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `domain` | `string \| null` | No | Target domain for authentication |
| `error_code` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Machine-readable error code (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `error_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Error message (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `external_action_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Instructions for external action (present when flow_step=awaiting_external_action) |
| `fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical fields awaiting input. Prefer this over discovered_fields when present. |
| `flow_expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the current flow expires (null when no flow in progress). A flow past this timestamp is no longer valid and its `flow_status` will be `EXPIRED`. Clients may start a new login to supersede a stale `IN_PROGRESS` flow past this timestamp. |
| `flow_status` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current flow status (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_step` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current step in the flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_type` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Type of the current flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `health_check_interval` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Interval in seconds between automatic health checks. When set, the system periodically verifies the authentication status and triggers re-authentication if needed. Maximum is 86400 (24 hours). Default is 3600 (1 hour) or your plan minimum, whichever is larger. The minimum depends on your plan: Enterprise: 300 (5 minutes), Startup: 1200 (20 minutes), Hobbyist: 3600 (1 hour), Free: 21600 (6 hours). |
| `health_check_unavailable_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Why health checks cannot verify this connection. Present when health checks are enabled but no auth check URL is available; a recent last_auth_check_at is not evidence of a valid session. |
| `health_checks` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether periodic health checks are enabled for this connection. When false, the system will not automatically verify authentication status, and `auto_reauth` has no effect on the automatic flow (since re-auth is only triggered by a failed scheduled health check). Manually triggering a health check via the API still works regardless of this setting. |
| `hitl` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether a login blocked by a CAPTCHA the browser cannot solve pauses at AWAITING_HUMAN_INTERVENTION so a person can complete it in the live view. |
| `hosted_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | URL to redirect user to for hosted login (present when flow in progress) |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the auth connection |
| `interaction_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Opaque identifier for the current canonical interaction. Required when submitting fields or choices and changes for each new actionable pause. |
| `last_auth_at` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated alias for `last_auth_check_at`. Despite the name, this is the last health-check timestamp, not the last successful authentication. Use `last_auth_check_at` instead. |
| `last_auth_check_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the most recent auth health check ran for this connection, regardless of outcome. Updated on every health check and does not by itself indicate that the profile is currently authenticated - use `status` for that. May be newer than `flow_expires_at` when a flow is still in progress because health checks continue to run in parallel. |
| `live_view_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Browser live view URL for debugging (present when flow in progress) |
| `login_url` | `string \| null` | No | Optional login page URL to skip discovery |
| `mfa_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | MFA method options (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `pending_sso_buttons` | `object[] \| null` | No | SSO buttons available (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `post_login_url` | `string \| null` | No | URL where the browser landed after successful login |
| `profile_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the profile associated with this auth connection |
| `proxy_id` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated. Read browser.proxy instead. Retained during migration for existing clients. |
| `record_session` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether to record browser session replays for this connection by default. Useful for debugging login flows. Can be overridden per-login. |
| `save_credentials` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether credentials are saved after every successful login. One-time codes (TOTP, SMS, etc.) are not saved. |
| `sign_in_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | Non-MFA choices presented during the auth flow, such as account selection or org pickers (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions). |
| `sso_provider` | `string \| null \| null` | No | SSO provider being used (e.g., google, github, microsoft) |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Last known authentication status of the managed profile. An inconclusive health check preserves this status and does not verify the current session. |
| `website_error` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Visible error message from the website (e.g., 'Incorrect password'). Present when the website displays an error during login. |

### connections.delete

`auth.connections.delete`

Delete a managed auth connection and stop its health checks and logins. Cannot be undone; the profile stays.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.delete({ id: 'auth_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### connections.exchange

`auth.connections.exchange`

Exchange a login handoff code for a short-lived token, as Kernel's hosted login page does.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.exchange({ id: 'auth_123', code: 'return 1;' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |
| `code` | `string` | Yes | Handoff code from start endpoint |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `invocation_id` | `string \| null` | No | Invocation ID |
| `jwt` | `string \| null` | No | JWT token with invocation_id claim (30 minute TTL) |



---

### connections.get

`auth.connections.get`

Get a managed auth connection and its current login flow: status, step, the fields it is waiting for, and hosted_url or live_view_url for a person to finish it. Poll this while a login runs.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.get({ id: 'auth_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `allowed_domains` | `string[] \| null` | No | Additional hostname roots valid for this auth flow, besides the primary domain. Each value allows credential entry on that exact hostname and its subdomains. Leading `www.` and `*.` labels are normalized away. When omitted or empty, credential entry is unrestricted. The following SSO/OAuth provider domains are automatically allowed by default and do not need to be specified: - Google: accounts.google.com - Microsoft/Azure AD: login.microsoftonline.com, login.live.com - Okta: *.okta.com, *.oktapreview.com - Auth0: *.auth0.com, *.us.auth0.com, *.eu.auth0.com, *.au.auth0.com - Apple: appleid.apple.com - GitHub: github.com - Facebook/Meta: www.facebook.com - LinkedIn: www.linkedin.com - Amazon Cognito: *.amazoncognito.com - OneLogin: *.onelogin.com - Ping Identity: *.pingone.com, *.pingidentity.com |
| `auto_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether automatic re-authentication is permitted for this connection. This is an opt-in flag only — it does not check whether re-auth is actually feasible. Even when true, re-auth only runs when the system has what it needs to perform it (for example, saved credentials for the required login fields), and only after a scheduled health check detects an expired session — so this flag has no effect when `health_checks` is false. When false, expired sessions detected by a health check are marked as `NEEDS_AUTH` instead of attempting re-auth. |
| `browser` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `browser_session_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | ID of the underlying browser session driving the current flow (present when flow in progress). Use this to inspect or terminate the browser session via the `/browsers` API. |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `can_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether this connection's stored requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication. A true value can represent either fully satisfiable requirements or a best-effort attempt. It does not account for whether automatic re-authentication is enabled or parent workflow state such as an active flow or circuit-breaker cooldown, so it does not guarantee an attempt on the next health check. See `can_reauth_reason` for the specific outcome. |
| `can_reauth_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Machine-readable reason for the current value of `can_reauth`. Affirmative values (requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication): - `external_credential` — an external credential provider is attached - `cua_has_credential` — CUA flow with a stored credential - `has_credential` — Kernel credential is attached (optimistic; plan viability not checked) - `viable_plans_found` — at least one stored login plan can be replayed - `no_requirements_recorded` — no recorded credential requirements to fail against - `totp_reauth_allowed` — TOTP is the only recorded requirement and a stored secret can generate the code - `optimistic_totp_attempt` — TOTP was previously required but no reusable secret is stored; the connection remains eligible for a circuit-breaker-bounded attempt because the site may not challenge returning sessions - `requirements_satisfiable` — recorded requirements contain no recognized blocker Negative values (a human must complete the login flow): - `no_prior_successful_login` — connection has never completed a successful login - `no_credential` — no Kernel or external credential attached - `no_viable_plans` — credential attached but no replayable login plan exists yet - `viable_plans_require_external_action` — stored plans need an external step (email link, push, etc.) - `requires_external_action` — recorded requirements include an external step - `requires_totp_without_secret` — flow needs a TOTP code but no TOTP secret is stored - `requires_sms_code` — flow needs an SMS code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_email_code` — flow needs an email code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_customer_input` — flow needs another field or choice that is unavailable during unattended re-authentication |
| `choices` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical choices awaiting selection. Prefer this over pending_sso_buttons, mfa_options, and sign_in_options when present. |
| `credential` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `discovered_fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Fields awaiting input (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `domain` | `string \| null` | No | Target domain for authentication |
| `error_code` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Machine-readable error code (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `error_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Error message (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `external_action_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Instructions for external action (present when flow_step=awaiting_external_action) |
| `fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical fields awaiting input. Prefer this over discovered_fields when present. |
| `flow_expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the current flow expires (null when no flow in progress). A flow past this timestamp is no longer valid and its `flow_status` will be `EXPIRED`. Clients may start a new login to supersede a stale `IN_PROGRESS` flow past this timestamp. |
| `flow_status` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current flow status (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_step` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current step in the flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_type` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Type of the current flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `health_check_interval` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Interval in seconds between automatic health checks. When set, the system periodically verifies the authentication status and triggers re-authentication if needed. Maximum is 86400 (24 hours). Default is 3600 (1 hour) or your plan minimum, whichever is larger. The minimum depends on your plan: Enterprise: 300 (5 minutes), Startup: 1200 (20 minutes), Hobbyist: 3600 (1 hour), Free: 21600 (6 hours). |
| `health_check_unavailable_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Why health checks cannot verify this connection. Present when health checks are enabled but no auth check URL is available; a recent last_auth_check_at is not evidence of a valid session. |
| `health_checks` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether periodic health checks are enabled for this connection. When false, the system will not automatically verify authentication status, and `auto_reauth` has no effect on the automatic flow (since re-auth is only triggered by a failed scheduled health check). Manually triggering a health check via the API still works regardless of this setting. |
| `hitl` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether a login blocked by a CAPTCHA the browser cannot solve pauses at AWAITING_HUMAN_INTERVENTION so a person can complete it in the live view. |
| `hosted_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | URL to redirect user to for hosted login (present when flow in progress) |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the auth connection |
| `interaction_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Opaque identifier for the current canonical interaction. Required when submitting fields or choices and changes for each new actionable pause. |
| `last_auth_at` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated alias for `last_auth_check_at`. Despite the name, this is the last health-check timestamp, not the last successful authentication. Use `last_auth_check_at` instead. |
| `last_auth_check_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the most recent auth health check ran for this connection, regardless of outcome. Updated on every health check and does not by itself indicate that the profile is currently authenticated - use `status` for that. May be newer than `flow_expires_at` when a flow is still in progress because health checks continue to run in parallel. |
| `live_view_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Browser live view URL for debugging (present when flow in progress) |
| `login_url` | `string \| null` | No | Optional login page URL to skip discovery |
| `mfa_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | MFA method options (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `pending_sso_buttons` | `object[] \| null` | No | SSO buttons available (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `post_login_url` | `string \| null` | No | URL where the browser landed after successful login |
| `profile_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the profile associated with this auth connection |
| `proxy_id` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated. Read browser.proxy instead. Retained during migration for existing clients. |
| `record_session` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether to record browser session replays for this connection by default. Useful for debugging login flows. Can be overridden per-login. |
| `save_credentials` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether credentials are saved after every successful login. One-time codes (TOTP, SMS, etc.) are not saved. |
| `sign_in_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | Non-MFA choices presented during the auth flow, such as account selection or org pickers (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions). |
| `sso_provider` | `string \| null \| null` | No | SSO provider being used (e.g., google, github, microsoft) |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Last known authentication status of the managed profile. An inconclusive health check preserves this status and does not verify the current session. |
| `website_error` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Visible error message from the website (e.g., 'Incorrect password'). Present when the website displays an error during login. |

### connections.list

`auth.connections.list`

List managed auth connections (site logins Kernel keeps signed in), filtered by profile or domain.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `profile_name` | `string` | No | Filter by profile name |
| `domain` | `string` | No | Filter by domain |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Maximum number of results to return |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Number of results to skip |
| `query` | `string` | No | Search auth connections by ID, domain, or profile name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

### connections.login

`auth.connections.login`

Start a login for a managed auth connection. Returns hosted_url (and live_view_url) for a person to complete it, or logs in by itself when credentials are stored. Then poll auth.connections.get, and send asked-for fields with auth.connections.submit.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.login({ id: 'conn_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |
| `browser` | `object` | No | — |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `proxy` | `object` | No | — |
| `record_session` | `boolean` | No | Override the connection's default for recording this login's browser session. When omitted, the connection's record_session default is used. |
| `skill_mode` | `enabled \| disabled` | No | Controls whether this login reads and writes learned domain skills. Automatic reauths inherit the selected mode until a later accepted login sets enabled or omits this field. Defaults to enabled when omitted. |

---

### connections.submit

`auth.connections.submit`

Send the values a running login asks for (a username, password, one-time code, or a choice) to a managed auth connection. Poll auth.connections.get for the result.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.submit({ id: 'auth_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |
| `field_values` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Canonical map of field ID to submitted value. |
| `fields` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Map of field name to value |
| `interaction_id` | `string` | No | Opaque interaction ID returned with canonical fields and choices. Required for canonical submissions. |
| `mfa_option_id` | `string` | No | The MFA method type to select (when mfa_options were returned) |
| `selected_choice_id` | `string` | No | Canonical choice ID selected by the user. |
| `sign_in_option_id` | `string` | No | The sign-in option ID to select (when sign_in_options were returned) |
| `sso_button_selector` | `string` | No | XPath selector for the SSO button to click (ODA). Use sso_provider instead for CUA. |
| `sso_provider` | `string` | No | SSO provider to click, matching the provider field from pending_sso_buttons (e.g., "google", "github"). Cannot be used with sso_button_selector. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `accepted` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether the submission was accepted for processing |



---

### connections.timeline

`auth.connections.timeline`

List a managed auth connection's history (logins, re-auths, health checks), newest first.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.timeline({ id: 'auth_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |
| `type` | `login \| reauth \| health_check` | No | Filter the timeline to a single event type. |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Maximum number of events to return |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Number of events to skip |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  browser_session_id?: string | null,
  completed_at?: string | null,
  error_code?: string | null,
  error_message?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  previous_status?: string | null,
  replay_id?: string | null,
  status?: string | null,
  step?: string | null,
  telemetry_captured?: boolean | null,
  timestamp?: string | null,
  type?: string | null,
  updated_at?: string | null,
  website_error?: string | null
}[]

### connections.update

`auth.connections.update`

Change a managed auth connection's settings: health checks, auto re-auth, credential, proxy, login URL.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.auth.connections.update({ id: 'auth_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Auth connection ID |
| `allowed_domains` | `string[]` | No | Additional hostname roots valid for this auth flow. Each value allows credential entry on that exact hostname and its subdomains; leading `www.` and `*.` labels are normalized away. An empty list leaves credential entry unrestricted. Replaces the existing list. |
| `auto_reauth` | `boolean` | No | Whether automatic re-authentication is permitted for this connection. This is an opt-in flag only — it does not check whether re-auth is actually feasible. Even when true, re-auth only runs when the system has what it needs to perform it (for example, saved credentials for the required login fields), and only after a scheduled health check detects an expired session — so this flag has no effect when `health_checks` is false. When false, expired sessions detected by a health check are marked as `NEEDS_AUTH` instead of attempting re-auth. |
| `browser` | `object` | No | — |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `credential` | `object` | No | — |
| `health_check_interval` | `number` | No | Interval in seconds between automatic health checks |
| `health_checks` | `boolean` | No | Whether periodic health checks are enabled. When set to false, the system will not automatically verify authentication status, and `auto_reauth` has no effect on the automatic flow (since re-auth is only triggered by a failed scheduled health check). |
| `hitl` | `boolean` | No | Whether a login blocked by a CAPTCHA the browser cannot solve pauses for a person instead of failing |
| `login_url` | `string` | No | Login page URL. Set to empty string to clear. |
| `proxy` | `object` | No | — |
| `record_session` | `boolean` | No | Whether to record browser sessions for this connection by default |
| `save_credentials` | `boolean` | No | Whether to save credentials after every successful login |

**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `allowed_domains` | `string[] \| null` | No | Additional hostname roots valid for this auth flow, besides the primary domain. Each value allows credential entry on that exact hostname and its subdomains. Leading `www.` and `*.` labels are normalized away. When omitted or empty, credential entry is unrestricted. The following SSO/OAuth provider domains are automatically allowed by default and do not need to be specified: - Google: accounts.google.com - Microsoft/Azure AD: login.microsoftonline.com, login.live.com - Okta: *.okta.com, *.oktapreview.com - Auth0: *.auth0.com, *.us.auth0.com, *.eu.auth0.com, *.au.auth0.com - Apple: appleid.apple.com - GitHub: github.com - Facebook/Meta: www.facebook.com - LinkedIn: www.linkedin.com - Amazon Cognito: *.amazoncognito.com - OneLogin: *.onelogin.com - Ping Identity: *.pingone.com, *.pingidentity.com |
| `auto_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether automatic re-authentication is permitted for this connection. This is an opt-in flag only — it does not check whether re-auth is actually feasible. Even when true, re-auth only runs when the system has what it needs to perform it (for example, saved credentials for the required login fields), and only after a scheduled health check detects an expired session — so this flag has no effect when `health_checks` is false. When false, expired sessions detected by a health check are marked as `NEEDS_AUTH` instead of attempting re-auth. |
| `browser` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `browser_session_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | ID of the underlying browser session driving the current flow (present when flow in progress). Use this to inspect or terminate the browser session via the `/browsers` API. |
| `browser_telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `can_reauth` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether this connection's stored requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication. A true value can represent either fully satisfiable requirements or a best-effort attempt. It does not account for whether automatic re-authentication is enabled or parent workflow state such as an active flow or circuit-breaker cooldown, so it does not guarantee an attempt on the next health check. See `can_reauth_reason` for the specific outcome. |
| `can_reauth_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Machine-readable reason for the current value of `can_reauth`. Affirmative values (requirements are eligible for unattended re-authentication): - `external_credential` — an external credential provider is attached - `cua_has_credential` — CUA flow with a stored credential - `has_credential` — Kernel credential is attached (optimistic; plan viability not checked) - `viable_plans_found` — at least one stored login plan can be replayed - `no_requirements_recorded` — no recorded credential requirements to fail against - `totp_reauth_allowed` — TOTP is the only recorded requirement and a stored secret can generate the code - `optimistic_totp_attempt` — TOTP was previously required but no reusable secret is stored; the connection remains eligible for a circuit-breaker-bounded attempt because the site may not challenge returning sessions - `requirements_satisfiable` — recorded requirements contain no recognized blocker Negative values (a human must complete the login flow): - `no_prior_successful_login` — connection has never completed a successful login - `no_credential` — no Kernel or external credential attached - `no_viable_plans` — credential attached but no replayable login plan exists yet - `viable_plans_require_external_action` — stored plans need an external step (email link, push, etc.) - `requires_external_action` — recorded requirements include an external step - `requires_totp_without_secret` — flow needs a TOTP code but no TOTP secret is stored - `requires_sms_code` — flow needs an SMS code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_email_code` — flow needs an email code that cannot be received automatically - `requires_customer_input` — flow needs another field or choice that is unavailable during unattended re-authentication |
| `choices` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical choices awaiting selection. Prefer this over pending_sso_buttons, mfa_options, and sign_in_options when present. |
| `credential` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `discovered_fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Fields awaiting input (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `domain` | `string \| null` | No | Target domain for authentication |
| `error_code` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Machine-readable error code (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `error_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Error message (present when flow_status=failed) |
| `external_action_message` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Instructions for external action (present when flow_step=awaiting_external_action) |
| `fields` | `object[] \| null` | No | Canonical fields awaiting input. Prefer this over discovered_fields when present. |
| `flow_expires_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When the current flow expires (null when no flow in progress). A flow past this timestamp is no longer valid and its `flow_status` will be `EXPIRED`. Clients may start a new login to supersede a stale `IN_PROGRESS` flow past this timestamp. |
| `flow_status` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current flow status (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_step` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Current step in the flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `flow_type` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Type of the current flow (null when no flow in progress) |
| `health_check_interval` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Interval in seconds between automatic health checks. When set, the system periodically verifies the authentication status and triggers re-authentication if needed. Maximum is 86400 (24 hours). Default is 3600 (1 hour) or your plan minimum, whichever is larger. The minimum depends on your plan: Enterprise: 300 (5 minutes), Startup: 1200 (20 minutes), Hobbyist: 3600 (1 hour), Free: 21600 (6 hours). |
| `health_check_unavailable_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Why health checks cannot verify this connection. Present when health checks are enabled but no auth check URL is available; a recent last_auth_check_at is not evidence of a valid session. |
| `health_checks` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether periodic health checks are enabled for this connection. When false, the system will not automatically verify authentication status, and `auto_reauth` has no effect on the automatic flow (since re-auth is only triggered by a failed scheduled health check). Manually triggering a health check via the API still works regardless of this setting. |
| `hitl` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether a login blocked by a CAPTCHA the browser cannot solve pauses at AWAITING_HUMAN_INTERVENTION so a person can complete it in the live view. |
| `hosted_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | URL to redirect user to for hosted login (present when flow in progress) |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the auth connection |
| `interaction_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Opaque identifier for the current canonical interaction. Required when submitting fields or choices and changes for each new actionable pause. |
| `last_auth_at` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated alias for `last_auth_check_at`. Despite the name, this is the last health-check timestamp, not the last successful authentication. Use `last_auth_check_at` instead. |
| `last_auth_check_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the most recent auth health check ran for this connection, regardless of outcome. Updated on every health check and does not by itself indicate that the profile is currently authenticated - use `status` for that. May be newer than `flow_expires_at` when a flow is still in progress because health checks continue to run in parallel. |
| `live_view_url` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Browser live view URL for debugging (present when flow in progress) |
| `login_url` | `string \| null` | No | Optional login page URL to skip discovery |
| `mfa_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | MFA method options (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `pending_sso_buttons` | `object[] \| null` | No | SSO buttons available (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions) |
| `post_login_url` | `string \| null` | No | URL where the browser landed after successful login |
| `profile_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the profile associated with this auth connection |
| `proxy_id` | `string \| null` | No | Deprecated. Read browser.proxy instead. Retained during migration for existing clients. |
| `record_session` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether to record browser session replays for this connection by default. Useful for debugging login flows. Can be overridden per-login. |
| `save_credentials` | `boolean \| null` | No | Whether credentials are saved after every successful login. One-time codes (TOTP, SMS, etc.) are not saved. |
| `sign_in_options` | `object[] \| null` | No | Non-MFA choices presented during the auth flow, such as account selection or org pickers (present when flow_step=awaiting_input; may also be present with awaiting_external_action as fallback actions). |
| `sso_provider` | `string \| null \| null` | No | SSO provider being used (e.g., google, github, microsoft) |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Last known authentication status of the managed profile. An inconclusive health check preserves this status and does not verify the current session. |
| `website_error` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Visible error message from the website (e.g., 'Incorrect password'). Present when the website displays an error during login. |

## Browser Pools

### acquire

`browserPools.acquire`

Take a ready browser from a pool (waits up to acquire_timeout_seconds). Returns null when none freed up in time; call again. Give it back with browserPools.release.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.acquire({ id_or_name: 'my-pool' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Browser pool ID or name |
| `acquire_timeout_seconds` | `number` | No | Maximum number of seconds to wait for a browser to be available. Defaults to the calculated time it would take to fill the pool at the currently configured fill rate. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Optional human-readable name for the acquired browser session, used to find it later in the dashboard. Must be unique among active sessions within the pool's project. Applies to this lease only and is cleared when the browser is released back to the pool. |
| `profile` | `object` | No | Optional profile to load before returning the acquired browser. The pool itself must not already have a profile configured. A browser loaded with an acquire-time profile is destroyed and replaced on release instead of being reused, so profile state cannot leak into another lease. Set save_changes to persist changes when that browser is destroyed. |
| `start_replay` | `boolean` | No | Start replay recording immediately for the acquired browser. Omit to use the pool's configured default. |
| `start_url` | `string` | No | Optional URL to navigate the acquired browser to. Overrides the pool's start_url for this acquire only. Best-effort: failures to navigate do not fail the acquire. |
| `tags` | `Record<string, string>` | No | — |
| `telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |

}

Captures what was requested, not what the browser actually loaded. |

| `viewport` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `webdriver_ws_url` | `string \| null` | No | Websocket URL for WebDriver BiDi connections to the browser session |

### create

`browserPools.create`

Create a pool of pre-started browsers with one configuration, for instant browserPools.acquire. Idle pool browsers are not billed but count toward the concurrency limit.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.create({ size: 1 });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `captcha_solver` | `object` | No | — |
| `chrome_policy` | `object` | No | Custom Chrome enterprise policy overrides applied to all browsers in this pool. Keys are Chrome enterprise policy names; values must match their expected types. Blocked: kernel-managed policies (extensions, proxy, CDP/automation). See https://chromeenterprise.google/policies/ The serialized JSON payload is capped at 5 MiB. |
| `extensions` | `object[]` | No | List of browser extensions to load into the session. Provide each by id or name. |
| `fill_rate_per_minute` | `number` | No | Percentage of the pool to fill per minute. Defaults to 25. The cap is 25 for most organizations but can be raised per-organization, so only the lower bound is enforced here. |
| `headless` | `boolean` | No | If true, launches the browser using a headless image. Defaults to false. |
| `kiosk_mode` | `boolean` | No | If true, launches the browser in kiosk mode to hide address bar and tabs in live view. Defaults to false. |
| `memory` | `1GiB \| 2GiB \| 4GiB \| 8GiB \| 16GiB` | No | — |
| `name` | `string` | No | Optional name for the browser pool. Must be unique within the project. |
| `network` | `object` | No | — |
| `profile` | `object` | No | Profile selection for browsers in the pool. |
| `proxy_id` | `string` | No | Optional proxy to associate to the browser session. Must reference a proxy in the same project as the browser session. |
| `refresh_on_profile_update` | `boolean` | No | When true, flush idle browsers when the profile the pool uses is updated, so pool browsers pick up the latest profile data. When a profile is provided during creation, this defaults to true. Requires a profile to be set on the pool. |
| `region` | `us-east \| us-west \| eu-west \| ap-southeast` | No | — |
| `size` | `number` | Yes | Number of browsers to maintain in the pool. The maximum size is determined by your organization's pooled sessions limit (the sum of all pool sizes cannot exceed your limit). |
| `start_replay` | `boolean` | No | Start replay recording automatically when a browser is acquired from this pool. |
| `start_url` | `string` | No | Optional URL to navigate to when a new browser is warmed into the pool. Best-effort: failures to navigate do not fail pool fill. Only applied to newly-warmed browsers; browsers reused via release/acquire keep whatever URL the previous lease left them on. Accepts any URL Chromium can resolve, including chrome:// pages. |
| `stealth` | `boolean` | No | If true, enables the CAPTCHA solver and, when proxy_id is omitted, uses Kernel's default stealth proxy. Defaults to false. Cannot be combined with captcha_solver.enabled=false. Deprecated in favor of captcha_solver and proxy_id. |
| `telemetry` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `timeout_seconds` | `number` | No | Default idle timeout in seconds for browsers acquired from this pool before they are destroyed. Defaults to 600 seconds. Minimum 10, maximum 259200 (72 hours). |
| `viewport` | `object` | No | Browser viewport used for newly-warmed browsers in this pool. |

Authoritative for programmatic consumers; the extensions inside `browser_pool_config` reflect the configured selector (echoed as sent on create). |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the browser pool |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Browser pool name, if set |
| `profile_id` | `string \| null` | No | Resolved profile ID the pool is attached to. Omitted when no profile is attached. Authoritative for programmatic consumers; the profile inside `browser_pool_config` reflects the configured selector (echoed as sent on create). |
| `region` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### delete

`browserPools.delete`

Delete a browser pool and its browsers. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.delete({ id_or_name: 'my-pool' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Browser pool ID or name |
| `force` | `boolean` | No | If true, force delete even if browsers are currently leased. Leased browsers will be terminated. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### flush

`browserPools.flush`

Delete a pool's idle browsers so it refills with fresh ones.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.flush({ id_or_name: 'my-pool' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Browser pool ID or name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |

### get

`browserPools.get`

Get a browser pool: configuration, size, and how many browsers are available or leased.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.get({ id_or_name: 'my-pool' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Browser pool ID or name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `acquired_count` | `number \| null` | No | Number of browsers currently acquired from the pool |
| `available_count` | `number \| null` | No | Number of browsers currently available in the pool |
| `browser_pool_config` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the browser pool was created |
| `extension_ids` | `string[] \| null` | No | Resolved extension IDs attached to the pool, in configured load order. Empty when no extensions are attached. Authoritative for programmatic consumers; the extensions inside `browser_pool_config` reflect the configured selector (echoed as sent on create). |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the browser pool |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Browser pool name, if set |
| `profile_id` | `string \| null` | No | Resolved profile ID the pool is attached to. Omitted when no profile is attached. Authoritative for programmatic consumers; the profile inside `browser_pool_config` reflects the configured selector (echoed as sent on create). |
| `region` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### list

`browserPools.list`

List browser pools and their sizes.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.browserPools.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `limit` | `number` | No | Limit the number of browser pools to return. |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Offset the number of browser pools to return. |
| `query` | `string` | No | Case-insensitive substring match against browser pool name. IDs match by exact value. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Exact-match filter on browser pool name using the database collation. In production, matching is case- and accent-insensitive. During the default-project migration, unscoped requests prefer a concrete default-project browser pool over a legacy unscoped browser pool with the same name. |
| `region` | `us-east \| us-west \| eu-west \| ap-southeast` | No | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |
