## Deployments

### create

`deployments.create`

Deploy a Kernel app from a zip (URL or base64) with its entrypoint, or from a source repository.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.deployments.create({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `entrypoint_rel_path` | `string` | No | Relative path to the entrypoint of the application |
| `env_vars` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Map of environment variables to set for the deployed application. Each key-value pair represents an environment variable. |
| `file` | `string \| object \| custom` | No | ZIP file containing the application source directory |
| `force` | `boolean` | No | Allow overwriting an existing app version |
| `region` | `aws.us-east-1a` | No | Region for deployment. Currently we only support "aws.us-east-1a" |
| `source` | `object` | No | — |
| `version` | `string` | No | Version of the application. Can be any string. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="file full type">

```ts
string | {
  url: string,
  filename?: string,
  contentType?: string
} | {
  base64: string,
  filename?: string,
  contentType?: string
} | custom
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="source full type">

```ts
{
  auth?: {
    method: github_token,
    token: string
  },
  entrypoint: string,
  path?: string,
  ref: string,
  type: github,
  url: string
}
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the deployment was created |
| `entrypoint_rel_path` | `string \| null` | No | Relative path to the application entrypoint |
| `env_vars` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | Environment variables configured for this deployment. Values are redacted for API key, OAuth, and managed-auth callers, which receive every key with an empty string value. Only dashboard sessions receive the actual values. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the deployment |
| `region` | `string \| null` | No | Deployment region code |
| `source_checksum` | `string \| null` | No | Hex-encoded SHA-256 checksum of the source archive. For file uploads, this hashes the uploaded archive; for GitHub-sourced deployments, this hashes the GitHub archive downloaded by the API. Omitted for deployments created before this field was recorded. |
| `source_path` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the subpath within the repository that was used as the deploy root. Omitted when the repo root was used or for file uploads. |
| `source_ref` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the git ref as requested at deploy time (branch, tag, or commit SHA — not resolved to a commit). Omitted for file uploads. |
| `source_type` | `string \| null` | No | Origin of the deployed source code. This is read-only response provenance; `file` indicates an uploaded archive and `github` indicates a repository fetched by the API. |
| `source_url` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the repository URL that was fetched. Omitted for file uploads. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Current status of the deployment |
| `status_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Status reason |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Timestamp when the deployment was last updated |



---

### delete

`deployments.delete`

Delete an app deployment. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.deployments.delete({ id: 'deployment_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Deployment ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### get

`deployments.get`

Get a deployment and its status. Poll this instead of the event stream.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.deployments.get({ id: 'deployment_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Deployment ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the deployment was created |
| `entrypoint_rel_path` | `string \| null` | No | Relative path to the application entrypoint |
| `env_vars` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | Environment variables configured for this deployment. Values are redacted for API key, OAuth, and managed-auth callers, which receive every key with an empty string value. Only dashboard sessions receive the actual values. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the deployment |
| `region` | `string \| null` | No | Deployment region code |
| `source_checksum` | `string \| null` | No | Hex-encoded SHA-256 checksum of the source archive. For file uploads, this hashes the uploaded archive; for GitHub-sourced deployments, this hashes the GitHub archive downloaded by the API. Omitted for deployments created before this field was recorded. |
| `source_path` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the subpath within the repository that was used as the deploy root. Omitted when the repo root was used or for file uploads. |
| `source_ref` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the git ref as requested at deploy time (branch, tag, or commit SHA — not resolved to a commit). Omitted for file uploads. |
| `source_type` | `string \| null` | No | Origin of the deployed source code. This is read-only response provenance; `file` indicates an uploaded archive and `github` indicates a repository fetched by the API. |
| `source_url` | `string \| null` | No | For GitHub-sourced deployments, the repository URL that was fetched. Omitted for file uploads. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Current status of the deployment |
| `status_reason` | `string \| null` | No | Status reason |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Timestamp when the deployment was last updated |



---

## Extensions

### delete

`extensions.delete`

Delete an uploaded extension. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.delete({ id_or_name: 'my-extension' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Extension ID or extension name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### download

`extensions.download`

Download an uploaded extension's zip. Returns a file.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.download({ id_or_name: 'my-extension' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Extension ID or name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |



---

### downloadFromChromeStore

`extensions.downloadFromChromeStore`

Download an extension from the Chrome Web Store as an unpacked zip, ready for extensions.upload. Returns a file.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.downloadFromChromeStore({ url: 'https://chromewebstore.google.com/detail/ublock-origin-lite/ddkjiahejlhfcafbddmgiahcphecmpfh' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `url` | `string` | Yes | Chrome Web Store URL for the extension. |
| `os` | `win \| mac \| linux` | No | Target operating system for the extension package. Defaults to linux. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |



---

### get

`extensions.get`

Get an uploaded extension's metadata.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.get({ id_or_name: 'my-extension' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Extension ID or name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `checksum` | `string \| null \| null` | No | SHA-256 checksum, encoded as lowercase hexadecimal, of the exact uploaded extension archive bytes. This is not a normalized checksum of the extension contents; archive metadata, file ordering, and compression can change the checksum for otherwise identical contents. Omitted for legacy rows and server-repackaged Chrome Web Store extensions. |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the extension was created |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the extension |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Timestamp when the extension was last used |
| `name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Optional, easier-to-reference name for the extension. Must be unique within the project. |
| `size_bytes` | `number \| null` | No | Size of the extension archive in bytes |



---

### list

`extensions.list`

List uploaded Chrome extensions.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `limit` | `number` | No | Limit the number of extensions to return. |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Offset the number of extensions to return. |
| `query` | `string` | No | Case-insensitive substring match against extension name. IDs match by exact value. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Exact-match filter on extension name using the database collation. In production, matching is case- and accent-insensitive. During the default-project migration, unscoped requests prefer a concrete default-project extension over a legacy unscoped extension with the same name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  checksum?: string | null | null,
  created_at?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  last_used_at?: string | null | null,
  name?: string | null | null,
  size_bytes?: number | null
}[]
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



---

### upload

`extensions.upload`

Upload a Chrome extension (a zip of the unpacked extension, as a URL or base64) to use in browsers by name.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.extensions.upload({ file: 'https://example.com/archive.zip' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `file` | `string \| object \| custom` | Yes | ZIP file containing the browser extension. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Optional unique name within the project to reference this extension. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="file full type">

```ts
string | {
  url: string,
  filename?: string,
  contentType?: string
} | {
  base64: string,
  filename?: string,
  contentType?: string
} | custom
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `checksum` | `string \| null \| null` | No | SHA-256 checksum, encoded as lowercase hexadecimal, of the exact uploaded extension archive bytes. This is not a normalized checksum of the extension contents; archive metadata, file ordering, and compression can change the checksum for otherwise identical contents. Omitted for legacy rows and server-repackaged Chrome Web Store extensions. |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the extension was created |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the extension |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Timestamp when the extension was last used |
| `name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Optional, easier-to-reference name for the extension. Must be unique within the project. |
| `size_bytes` | `number \| null` | No | Size of the extension archive in bytes |

## Invocations

### create

`invocations.create`

Run an action of a deployed Kernel app (billed per second). async: true returns at once; poll invocations.get for the result.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.invocations.create({ app_name: 'my-app', version: '1', action_name: 'run' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `action_name` | `string` | Yes | Name of the action to invoke |
| `app_name` | `string` | Yes | Name of the application |
| `async` | `boolean` | No | If true, invoke asynchronously. When set, the API responds 202 Accepted with status "queued". |
| `async_timeout_seconds` | `number` | No | Timeout in seconds for async invocations (min 10, max 3600). Only applies when async is true. |
| `payload` | `string` | No | Input data for the action, sent as a JSON string. |
| `version` | `string` | Yes | Version of the application |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `action_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the action invoked |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | ID of the invocation |
| `output` | `string \| null` | No | The action result or detailed failure output. Often a JSON-encoded value, but failures may contain plain text. May contain sensitive application data. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Status of the invocation |
| `status_reason` | `string \| null` | No | A nonempty, customer-safe summary of the recorded failure output, always present when status is failed and omitted otherwise. Recognized messages receive a specific summary; other failures receive a generic summary. Message matching does not establish whether the failure originated in the platform or action code. Does not include raw action output or internal error details. Human-readable text, not a stable identifier for retry logic. |



---

### deleteBrowsers

`invocations.deleteBrowsers`

Delete every browser an invocation started. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.invocations.deleteBrowsers({ id: 'invocation_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Invocation ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### get

`invocations.get`

Get an invocation: status, output and timing. Poll this instead of the event stream.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.invocations.get({ id: 'invocation_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | The invocation ID |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `action_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the action invoked |
| `app_name` | `string \| null` | No | Name of the application |
| `finished_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | RFC 3339 Nanoseconds timestamp when the invocation finished (null if still running) |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | ID of the invocation |
| `output` | `string \| null` | No | The action result or detailed failure output. Often a JSON-encoded value, but failures may contain plain text. May contain sensitive application data. |
| `payload` | `string \| null` | No | Payload provided to the invocation. This is a string that can be parsed as JSON. |
| `started_at` | `string \| null` | No | RFC 3339 Nanoseconds timestamp when the invocation started |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Status of the invocation |
| `status_reason` | `string \| null` | No | A nonempty, customer-safe summary of the recorded failure output, always present when status is failed and omitted otherwise, including in the first failed invocation_state event. Recognized messages receive a specific summary; other failures receive a generic summary. Message matching does not establish whether the failure originated in the platform or action code. Does not include raw action output or internal error details. Available for historical invocations as well. Human-readable text, not a stable identifier for retry logic. |
| `version` | `string \| null` | No | Version label for the application |



---

### list

`invocations.list`

List app action invocations, filtered by app, action, status or time.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.invocations.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `app_name` | `string` | No | Filter results by application name. |
| `version` | `string` | No | Filter results by application version. |
| `action_name` | `string` | No | Filter results by action name. |
| `deployment_id` | `string` | No | Filter results by deployment ID. |
| `status` | `queued \| running \| succeeded \| failed` | No | Filter results by invocation status. |
| `since` | `string` | No | Show invocations that have started since the given time (RFC timestamps or durations like 5m). |
| `limit` | `number` | No | Limit the number of invocations to return. |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Offset the number of invocations to return. |
| `query` | `string` | No | Search invocations by ID, app name, or action name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  action_name?: string | null,
  app_name?: string | null,
  finished_at?: string | null | null,
  id?: string | null,
  output?: string | null,
  payload?: string | null,
  started_at?: string | null,
  status?: string | null,
  status_reason?: string | null,
  version?: string | null
}[]
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

```ts
  max_concurrent_invocations?: number | null,
  max_vaults?: number | null | null
} | null
```

```ts
  id?: string | null,

  status?: string | null | null,

} | null
```
</Accordion>

### limits.get

`organization.limits.get`

Get the org's limits and current use: concurrent browsers, managed auth connections, vaults.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.organization.limits.get({});
```

**Input:** _empty object_


**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `auth_connections_used` | `number \| null` | No | The organization's current non-deleted managed auth connections, counted org-wide across every project. Compare against max_auth_connections to show remaining capacity before a create is rejected with 403 insufficient_plan. |
| `concurrent_sessions_available` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Number of concurrent browser slots currently available to the organization. This is the effective concurrency limit minus active on-demand sessions and browser pool reservations, floored at zero. Null when usage cannot be read. |
| `concurrent_sessions_used` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Current organization-wide concurrent browser usage, including active on-demand sessions and browser pool reservations. Null when usage cannot be read. |
| `default_project_max_concurrent_sessions` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Default maximum concurrent browsers applied to every project that has no explicit per-project override. Null means no org-level default, so such projects are uncapped (only the org-wide limit applies). Applies to existing and newly created projects. |
| `max_auth_connections` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Maximum managed auth connections the organization's plan allows. Null means unlimited. Counted org-wide, so it cannot be multiplied across projects. |
| `max_concurrent_sessions` | `number \| null` | No | The organization's effective concurrency limit — the maximum browsers running at once, covering both on-demand sessions and browser pool reservations — from its plan or an override. Read-only and shared across all projects in the org; a per-project default cannot exceed it. |
| `max_vaults` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Maximum non-deleted vaults allowed org-wide across all projects. Null means unlimited. |
| `min_health_check_interval_seconds` | `number \| null` | No | Smallest health_check_interval the organization's plan accepts on a managed auth connection. Requests below this are rejected with 400. Existing connections stored below the floor are grandfathered until edited. |
| `vaults_used` | `number \| null` | No | Current non-deleted vault count across all projects in the organization. |

Null means no org-level default, so such projects are uncapped (only the org-wide limit applies). Applies to existing and newly created projects. |
| `max_auth_connections` | `number \| null \| null` | No | Maximum managed auth connections the organization's plan allows. Null means unlimited. Counted org-wide, so it cannot be multiplied across projects. |

Read-only and shared across all projects in the org; a per-project default cannot exceed it. |

## Profiles

### create

`profiles.create`

Create an empty browser profile (saved cookies and storage) to start browsers signed in. Fill it by starting a browser with profile &#123;name, save_changes: true&#125;, logging in, then deleting the browser.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.profiles.create({ name: 'acme-login' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `name` | `string` | No | Optional name of the profile. Must be unique within the logical project; during the default-project migration, unscoped profiles and profiles in the org default project are treated as the same project. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the profile was created |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the profile |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the profile was last used |
| `name` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Optional, easier-to-reference name for the profile |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp when the profile was last updated |



---

### delete

`profiles.delete`

Delete a browser profile and its saved cookies and storage. Cannot be undone.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.profiles.delete({ id_or_name: 'acme-login' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Profile ID or profile name |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |



---

### download

`profiles.download`

Download a profile's saved state as a tar.zst (or tar) archive. Returns a file. Holds session cookies: treat it as a credential.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.profiles.download({ id_or_name: 'acme-login' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Profile ID or name |
| `format` | `tar.zst \| tar` | No | Response format for current profile archives. Legacy profiles are always returned as JSON. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `contentBase64` | `string` | No | The file, base64-encoded. Missing when the file came back as text or contentOmitted is set. |
| `text` | `string` | No | The file as text, in place of contentBase64, when it is a text file (CSV, JSON, Markdown, XML and the like) in UTF-8. |
| `contentType` | `string` | Yes | Media type of the file, for example application/pdf. |
| `byteLength` | `number` | Yes | Size of the file in bytes. |
| `filename` | `string` | No | The file name, when known. |
| `contentOmitted` | `not_requested \| over_limit` | No | Set when the content was left out: the caller did not ask for it, or the file was over the limit. |
| `fileId` | `string` | No | Where the host saved the file. Over the limit it comes in place of the content; a host that keeps every file sets it beside the content too. On Corsair it is a Files id (fil_…): pass it to another app wherever that app takes a file URL, or read a text file or PDF with files.read_text. |

### list

`profiles.list`

List saved browser profiles (name, id, last used).

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.profiles.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `limit` | `number` | No | Limit the number of profiles to return. |
| `offset` | `number` | No | Offset the number of profiles to return. |
| `query` | `string` | No | Case-insensitive substring match against profile name or ID. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Exact-match filter on profile name using the database collation. In production, matching is case- and accent-insensitive. During the default-project migration, unscoped requests prefer a concrete default-project profile over a legacy unscoped profile with the same name. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

## Proxies

### check

`proxies.check`

Check that a proxy works and see its exit IP.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.proxies.check({ id_or_name: 'my-proxy' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | Proxy ID. Name-based addressing is supported only for retrieval. |
| `url` | `string` | No | An optional URL to test reachability against. If provided, the proxy check will test connectivity to this URL instead of the default test URLs. Only HTTP and HTTPS schemes are allowed, and the URL must resolve to a public IP address. For ISP proxies, the exit IP is stable, so a successful check reliably indicates that subsequent browser sessions will reach the target site with the same IP. For residential and mobile proxies, the exit node changes between requests, so a successful check validates proxy configuration but does not guarantee that a subsequent browser session will use the same exit IP or reach the same site — it is useful for verifying credentials and connectivity, not for predicting site-specific behavior. When provided, the check result does not update the proxy's health status, since a failure may indicate a problem with the target site rather than the proxy itself. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `bypass_hosts` | `string[] \| null` | No | Hostnames that should bypass the parent proxy and connect directly. |
| `config` | `object \| null` | No | Configuration specific to the selected proxy `type`. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `ip_address` | `string \| null` | No | IP address that the proxy uses when making requests. |
| `last_checked` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp of the last health check performed on this proxy. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Readable name of the proxy. |
| `protocol` | `string \| null` | No | Protocol to use for the proxy connection. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Current health status of the proxy. |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | Proxy type to use. In terms of quality for avoiding bot-detection, from best to worst: `mobile` &gt; `residential` &gt; `isp`. `datacenter` is deprecated; use `isp` instead. Existing datacenter proxies remain supported. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="config full type">

```ts
{
  country?: string | null
} | {
  country?: string | null,
  ip_address?: string | null
} | {
  asn?: string | null,
  city?: string | null,
  country?: string | null,
  os?: string | null,
  state?: string | null,
  zip?: string | null
} | {
  city?: string | null,
  country?: string | null,
  state?: string | null
} | {
  has_ca_bundle?: boolean | null,
  has_password?: boolean | null,
  host?: string | null,
  port?: number | null,
  username?: string | null
} | null
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>

### create

`proxies.create`

Create a proxy configuration (datacenter, ISP, residential, mobile or your own) for browsers to route through.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.proxies.create({ type: 'datacenter' });
```

**Input**

| `config` | `object` | No | Configuration specific to the selected proxy `type`. |
| `name` | `string` | No | Readable name of the proxy. |
| `protocol` | `http \| https` | No | Protocol to use for the proxy connection. |
| `type` | `datacenter \| isp \| residential \| mobile \| custom` | Yes | Proxy type to use. In terms of quality for avoiding bot-detection, from best to worst: `mobile` &gt; `residential` &gt; `isp`. `datacenter` is deprecated; use `isp` instead. Datacenter proxy creation remains supported for backward compatibility. |

| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Readable name of the proxy. |
| `protocol` | `string \| null` | No | Protocol to use for the proxy connection. |
| `status` | `string \| null` | No | Current health status of the proxy. |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | Proxy type to use. In terms of quality for avoiding bot-detection, from best to worst: `mobile` &gt; `residential` &gt; `isp`. `datacenter` is deprecated; use `isp` instead. Existing datacenter proxies remain supported. |

## Reports

### create

`reports.create`

Submit a typed report against a task (validated against the report type's schema).

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.reports.create({
	task_id: 'task_123',
	type: 'my-report-type',
	data: { key: 'value' },
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Makes retrying the create safe. A retry with the same key and the same request returns the record the first call created, with status 200, instead of creating another. Reusing a key with a different request returns 422. Keys are scoped to the project and do not expire. The Kernel SDKs send a key automatically and reuse it when they retry. |
| `data` | `object` | Yes | Report data. It must match the schema of the report type's version and encode to at most 16 KB of JSON. |
| `occurred_at` | `string` | No | When the reported event happened. Defaults to the time the report is submitted. Must be no more than 5 minutes in the future or 30 days in the past. |
| `schema_version` | `number` | No | Version of the report type's schema to validate data against. Defaults to the type's latest version, which the response returns. |
| `session_id` | `string` | No | ID of the browser the report was observed in. It must belong to the task's project; deleted browsers are accepted. The browser is linked to the task if it is not already. |
| `task_id` | `string` | Yes | ID of the task to report against. |
| `type` | `string` | Yes | Report type name. List report types to see the available types and their schemas. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="data full type">

```ts
{
}
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the report was submitted |
| `data` | `object \| null` | No | Report data, matching the report type's schema |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the report |
| `occurred_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the reported event happened |
| `schema_version` | `number \| null` | No | Version of the report type's schema that data was validated against |
| `session_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | ID of the browser the report was observed in, if any |
| `task_id` | `string \| null` | No | ID of the task the report belongs to |
| `type` | `string \| null` | No | Report type name |

### create

`search.create`

Search the web through one of Kernel's search providers and get ranked results (titles, URLs, snippets), kept for search.contents.fetch.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.search.create({ query: 'kernel browser automation' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `content` | `true \| object` | No | Optional portable content retrieval. Pass true for defaults or an options object. Omission never starts Kernel browser work; provider-supplied content is still returned when available, including when requested through native options. Both inline and deferred retrieval use the same options schema. |
| `country` | `string` | No | ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 search locale preference. |
| `end_date` | `string` | No | Inclusive publication-date upper bound; must not precede start_date. If recency is also supplied, recency takes precedence with a warning. Unsupported or approximated filtering is reported, or rejected under strict_params. |
| `exclude_domains` | `string[]` | No | Hostname exclusions, with the same best-effort/strict behavior as include_domains. Provider-specific combinations that cannot be represented are reported via warnings or rejected in strict mode. |
| `include_domains` | `string[]` | No | Hostname inclusion preference, matching a hostname and its subdomains. Empty means unrestricted. Translated, emulated, or dropped with a warning according to provider capability unless strict_params is true. Native boost modes remain advisory and are identified in warnings. |
| `include_raw` | `boolean` | No | Include untouched per-result payloads and the full serving-provider response in raw fields. Off by default; native top-level outputs such as answer remain available without it. |
| `language` | `string` | No | BCP 47 search language preference. |
| `max_results` | `number` | No | Requested result count from 1 through 100. The effective count is clamped to the serving provider's cap with a warning. Effective native counts are the lower of this limit and supplied provider-native count aliases. Strict mode rejects unsupported counts. |
| `query` | `string` | Yes | Primary search query. A provider-native multi-query option applies only to that provider; other providers in a fallback chain receive this query. |
| `recency` | `hour \| day \| week \| month \| year` | No | Relative search window. Takes precedence over start_date/end_date with a warning if both are set. Provider-native recency behavior is retained, including documented hour-to-day widening. Unsupported filters are rejected only in strict mode. |
| `safe_search` | `off \| moderate \| strict` | No | Optional safety preference. Omit to use provider defaults. Unsupported values are dropped with a warning unless strict_params is true. A search filter is not an authorization boundary. |
| `start_date` | `string` | No | Inclusive publication-date lower bound. If recency is also supplied, recency takes precedence with a warning. Provider date semantics, precision, and unsupported filters are reported; unknown source dates are not fabricated or universally post-filtered. |
| `strategy` | `object` | No | — |
| `strict_params` | `boolean` | No | When false, unsupported portable parameters are omitted and approximations are described in warnings. When true, every supplied portable parameter must be honored exactly. Requests that cannot be served with those parameters are rejected. This does not guarantee identical rankings or document timestamps across indexes. Authentication and project isolation are always enforced. |
| `timeout_ms` | `number` | No | Overall deadline across search attempts and inline retrieval. No new attempt starts after the deadline. Completed search results survive inline retrieval timeouts. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="content full type">

```ts
true | {
  browser?: {
    browser_id?: string,
    mode?: curl | render
  },
  format?: markdown | text,
  max_age_hours?: number,
  max_chars?: number,
  source?: auto | provider | browser,
  timeout_ms?: number
}
```
</Accordion>

| `results` | `object[] \| null` | No | — |
| `usage` | `object \| null` | No | — |

## Service Accounts

### create

`serviceAccounts.create`

Create a service account for workload identity sign-in.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.serviceAccounts.create({ name: 'my-name' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `description` | `string` | No | — |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `archived_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | — |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `created_by_user_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | — |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `last_used_at` | `string \| null \| null` | No | When one of the account's API keys last authenticated or one of its rules last issued a token. Recorded at most once every 15 minutes, so it can lag. Null if the account has never been used. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `org_id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `security_version` | `number \| null` | No | Authority revision. Cosmetic updates do not widen permissions. |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |



---

### delete

`serviceAccounts.delete`

Archive a service account; its sign-ins stop working.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.serviceAccounts.delete({ id: 'serviceAccount_123' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id` | `string` | Yes | Stable resource ID. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `success` | `true` | Yes | Kernel accepted the call; it answers with no body. |

## Tasks

### create

`tasks.create`

Record a task an agent is working on, optionally with the browsers it uses; submit reports against it.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.tasks.create({ description: 'example' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `idempotency_key` | `string` | No | Makes retrying the create safe. A retry with the same key and the same request returns the record the first call created, with status 200, instead of creating another. Reusing a key with a different request returns 422. Keys are scoped to the project and do not expire. The Kernel SDKs send a key automatically and reuse it when they retry. |
| `description` | `string` | Yes | What the task is meant to accomplish, at most 4 KB. |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string>` | No | — |
| `session_ids` | `string[]` | No | IDs of browsers to link to the task. Each must belong to the request's project; deleted browsers can be linked. |
| `started_at` | `string` | No | When work on the task started. Defaults to the time the task is created. Must be no more than 5 minutes in the future or 30 days in the past. |
| `target_website` | `string` | No | Hostname of the website the task targets, without a scheme or path. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | When the task was created |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | What the task is meant to accomplish |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the task |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | — |
| `session_ids` | `string[] \| null` | No | IDs of browsers linked to the task, oldest link first. Linked browsers may since have been deleted. |
| `started_at` | `string \| null` | No | When work on the task started |
| `target_website` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Hostname of the website the task targets |



---

Get a task.

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.tasks.get({ id: 'task_123' });
```

**Input**

| `description` | `string \| null` | No | What the task is meant to accomplish |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | Unique identifier for the task |
| `metadata` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | — |
| `session_ids` | `string[] \| null` | No | IDs of browsers linked to the task, oldest link first. Linked browsers may since have been deleted. |
| `started_at` | `string \| null` | No | When work on the task started |
| `target_website` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Hostname of the website the task targets |

Link browser sessions to a task.

```ts
});
```

| Name | Type | Required | Description |

| `started_at` | `string \| null` | No | When work on the task started |
| `target_website` | `string \| null \| null` | No | Hostname of the website the task targets |

## Telemetry

### destinations.create

`telemetry.destinations.create`

Add an OTLP destination that browser sessions export telemetry to.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.telemetry.destinations.create({ name: 'my-name', endpoint: 'example' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `description` | `string` | No | — |
| `endpoint` | `string` | Yes | Base endpoint of the OTLP/HTTP collector, without a signal path. Kernel appends the signal path itself, so pass `https://api.honeycomb.io` rather than `https://api.honeycomb.io/v1/logs`. If your provider's docs give you a signal-specific URL, drop the trailing `/v1/logs`, `/v1/traces`, or `/v1/metrics` — an endpoint that already carries one is rejected. Must be http or https, must resolve to a public address, and must carry no query string or fragment. Examples: `https://api.honeycomb.io`, `https://otlp-gateway-prod-us-east-0.grafana.net/otlp`, `https://otlp.datadoghq.com` (Datadog's OTLP intake for US1, not its logs intake). |
| `headers` | `Record<string, string>` | No | Headers sent with each export request, typically an ingestion key. Encrypted at rest and returned redacted. Names and values must be valid HTTP header tokens, and the names and values together cannot exceed 8192 bytes. Names are matched case-insensitively and stored canonicalized, so supplying two spellings of one header is rejected. |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | Unique within the organization. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `consecutive_failures` | `number \| null` | No | Failed deliveries since the last success, as observed by the relay process that wrote the latest outcome. Zero means the most recently recorded outcome succeeded. |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `endpoint` | `string \| null` | No | OTLP/HTTP endpoint telemetry is sent to. |
| `headers` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | Headers sent with each export request. Names are returned in canonical form (`Authorization`, not `authorization`). Non-dashboard reads return values redacted as empty strings, so the keys are visible but the credentials are not. Dashboard reads return the stored values. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `last_error` | `string \| null` | No | Sanitized class of the delivery failure recorded at `last_error_at`. It is retained after a later success, so its presence does not mean the destination is currently failing. Response bodies, endpoint URLs, credentials, and raw transport errors are never returned. |
| `last_error_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp of the most recent failed delivery. It is retained after a later success, so it can predate `last_export_at`. Read `consecutive_failures` to tell whether the destination is currently failing. |
| `last_export_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp of the most recent successful delivery. Moves only on success. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Unique within the organization. Usable in place of the ID when selecting a destination, so it cannot be shaped like an ID. |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |



---

### destinations.update

`telemetry.destinations.update`

Update an OTLP telemetry destination.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.telemetry.destinations.update({ id_or_name: 'my-destination' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | OTLP destination ID or name |
| `description` | `string` | No | — |
| `endpoint` | `string` | No | Base endpoint of the OTLP/HTTP collector, without a signal path. Same rules as on create. |
| `headers` | `Record<string, string \| null>` | No | Edits stored headers key by key rather than replacing the map. A string value adds or replaces that header, `null` deletes it, and any key you omit is left as it is. Names are matched case-insensitively, so `authorization` replaces a stored `Authorization` rather than adding a second entry. This is the credential rotation path; sessions already exporting pick up the new values without restarting. Names and values must be valid HTTP header tokens, and the names and values together cannot exceed 8192 bytes. |
| `name` | `string` | No | — |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `consecutive_failures` | `number \| null` | No | Failed deliveries since the last success, as observed by the relay process that wrote the latest outcome. Zero means the most recently recorded outcome succeeded. |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `endpoint` | `string \| null` | No | OTLP/HTTP endpoint telemetry is sent to. |
| `headers` | `Record<string, string> \| null` | No | Headers sent with each export request. Names are returned in canonical form (`Authorization`, not `authorization`). Non-dashboard reads return values redacted as empty strings, so the keys are visible but the credentials are not. Dashboard reads return the stored values. |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `last_error` | `string \| null` | No | Sanitized class of the delivery failure recorded at `last_error_at`. It is retained after a later success, so its presence does not mean the destination is currently failing. Response bodies, endpoint URLs, credentials, and raw transport errors are never returned. |
| `last_error_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp of the most recent failed delivery. It is retained after a later success, so it can predate `last_export_at`. Read `consecutive_failures` to tell whether the destination is currently failing. |
| `last_export_at` | `string \| null` | No | Timestamp of the most recent successful delivery. Moves only on success. |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Unique within the organization. Usable in place of the ID when selecting a destination, so it cannot be shaped like an ID. |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |

### items.get

`vaults.items.get`

Get a vault item and the operations it currently offers (wait up to 60 s for a change).

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.items.get({ id_or_name: 'my-vault', key: 'my-item' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `key` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `wait` | `number` | No | Hold for up to this many seconds while the item is pending authorization, approval, or credential collection. Return the current item when ready or when the wait elapses. This does not wait for edits to an already-ready credential; poll GET without wait and compare version to observe changes after collect. Managed auth credentials are created ready, so wait returns immediately. |
| `expand` | `(payment_methods \| link_user_info \| link_shipping_addresses)[]` | No | Live fields advertised by `available_expansions` to include in `expanded`. |

```ts
  action?: {

    url?: string | null
  } | {

    name?: string | null,
    url?: string | null
  } | {
    name?: string | null,
    url?: string | null
  } | {
    name?: string | null
  } | {

  } | {

  } | {

  } | {
```

---

### items.list

`vaults.items.list`

List a vault's items (no secret values).

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.items.list({ id_or_name: 'my-vault' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `id_or_name` | `string` | Yes | — |



**Output:** `object[]`

---

### items.performOperation

`vaults.items.performOperation`

Run an operation a vault item offers, such as fill, authorize, confirm_transaction or add_payment_method. Some move money or use a card: confirm with the person first.

**Risk:** `destructive` · **Irreversible**

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.items.performOperation({ id_or_name: 'my-vault', key: 'my-item', type: 'authorize' });
```

**Input:** `object`

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="Input full type">


---

### items.update

`vaults.items.update`

Update a vault item's card specification or credential values.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.items.update({
	id_or_name: 'my-vault',
	key: 'my-item',
	spec: {
		provider: 'link',
		wallet: 'example',
		payment_method_id: 'example',
		amount: 1,
		currency: 'exa',
		merchant_name: 'example',
		merchant_url: 'example',
		context: 'examplexxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx',
	},
});
```

**Input:** `object`

---

### items.upsert

`vaults.items.upsert`

Create a vault item (card, credential, wallet or payment request) under a key, or get the identical existing one.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.items.upsert({
	id_or_name: 'my-vault',
	key: 'team-wallet',
	type: 'wallet',
	spec: { provider: 'kernel' },
});
```

**Input:** `object`

### list

`vaults.list`

List vaults in the project.

**Risk:** `read`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.list({});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `limit` | `number` | No | — |
| `offset` | `number` | No | — |
| `query` | `string` | No | Case-insensitive substring match against vault name. IDs match by exact value. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `items` | `object[]` | Yes | This page of results. |
| `has_more` | `boolean` | Yes | True when more results follow; pass next_offset as offset to read them. |
| `next_offset` | `number \| null` | Yes | Value for `offset` that reads the next page, or null on the last page. |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="items full type">

```ts
{
  created_at?: string | null,
  id?: string | null,
  name?: string | null,
  updated_at?: string | null
}[]
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



---

### upsert

`vaults.upsert`

Create a vault (a project-scoped store of secrets, cards and credentials for browsers to use), or get the existing one with that name.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.vaults.upsert({ name: 'my-name' });
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `name` | `string` | Yes | Immutable name used to create or retrieve the vault. |



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `id` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `name` | `string \| null` | No | Immutable name assigned when the vault is created. |
| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |

## Workload Identity Federation Rules

### create

`workloadIdentityFederationRules.create`

Create a rule mapping an issuer's token claims to a service account.

**Risk:** `write`

```ts
await corsair.kernel.api.workloadIdentityFederationRules.create({
	name: 'my-name',
	issuer_id: 'example',
	match: { type: 'github_actions' },
	expected_audience: 'example',
	target: { type: 'service_account', service_account_id: 'example' },
	authorization: {
		project_id: 'example',
		policy: {
			statements: [{ action: 'example', resource: 'example' }],
		},
		token_audience: 'example',
	},
});
```

**Input**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|
| `authorization` | `object` | Yes | — |
| `description` | `string` | No | — |
| `enabled` | `boolean` | No | — |
| `expected_audience` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `issuer_id` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `match` | `object` | Yes | — |
| `name` | `string` | Yes | — |
| `target` | `object` | Yes | — |

<AccordionGroup>
<Accordion title="authorization full type">

```ts
{
  max_token_ttl_seconds?: number,
  policy: {
    statements: {
      action: string,
      condition?: {
        key: source_ip,
        operator: in,
        values: string[]
      },
      resource: string
    }[]
  },
  project_id: string,
  token_audience: string
}
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="match full type">

```ts
{
  claims?: Record<string, string>,
  environment?: string,
  event_names?: string[],
  job_workflow_ref?: string,
  namespace?: string,
  ref?: string,
  repository?: string,
  repository_id?: string,
  repository_owner_id?: string,
  service_account?: string,
  subject?: string,
  subject_prefix?: string,
  type: github_actions | aws | google_cloud | microsoft_entra | kubernetes | custom_oidc,
  workflow?: string,
  workflow_ref?: string
}
```
</Accordion>

<Accordion title="target full type">

```ts
{
  service_account_id: string,
  type: service_account
}
```
</Accordion>
</AccordionGroup>



**Output**

| Name | Type | Required | Description |
|------|------|----------|-------------|

| `authorization` | `object \| null` | No | — |
| `created_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
| `created_by_user_id` | `string \| null \| null` | No | — |
| `description` | `string \| null` | No | — |

| `expected_audience` | `string \| null` | No | — |

| `updated_at` | `string \| null` | No | — |
